When a product serves as a point of sale, customers can use their smartphone to scan the product to access an online store where they can buy other things or extras. “Things” can take care of themselves via object self-service; for example, a heating system can order a fluid refill when necessary. Finally, continuous sensor internet connectivity for remote use and condition assessment provides real-time information that, when needed, can trigger corrective actions. [Table 6.2](#ch06.xhtml#_24032-0872_ch006_tab_002) shows several business model patterns formed by combining digital and physical elements. It highlights tactics like freemium offerings, digital improvements, users’ limitations, and IoT-enabled features to increase customer value and revenue prospects.

## 6.5 Major Key Issues and Challenges of IoT

Substantial complexity is added due to the prevalence of Internet connectivity for various systems in every facet of human lives and various techniques involved in data transfer among embedded devices. IoT developers have several problems and difficulties to deal with, especially as society adopts more sophisticated smart technology. The difficulties and demand for more advanced IoT systems also grow as technology evolves. IoT developers should cultivate foresight by anticipating new problems and proactively collaborate to generate creative solutions to address them.

### 6.5.1 IoT Ethical Concerns

The biggest problem facing businesses is that consumers don’t fully comprehend how their IoT goods operate when they use them. In addition, it is widely believed that IoT security is woefully inadequate and highly vulnerable to cyberattacks. According to a Forbes study, the number of cyberattacks using IoT devices has increased by 300% in 2019 \[[21](#ch06.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp006_ref21)\]. This was due to rapidly rising number of gadgets and an absence of emphasis on safety rather than creativity.

 123.  

## 6.6 AI and ML

The design and development of sophisticated computer systems that can carry out tasks that normally require human intelligence is known as artificial intelligence (AI). Speech recognition, visual perception, making choices, language comprehension, translation, and independent problem-solving constitute a few of these activities. AI systems are becoming more and more capable of adapting, learning, and functioning on their own, which makes them adaptable to a range of settings. To be able to go through data, gain insights from it, and use this knowledge to carry out duties effectively, technological businesses are developing software and hardware that uses AI \[[22](#ch06.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp006_ref22), [23](#ch06.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp006_ref23)\]. Organisations are investing significantly in AI, from chatbot and quantum computing to driverless cars, to boost productivity and produce faster and more accurate results than with traditional human-driven approaches.

### 6.6.1 New Developments in AI

#### 6.6.1.1 Siri

One of Apple’s most well-known assistant programs Siri is accessible on the majority of their electronic products, including the iPad and phone. Individuals commonly engage with the female controlled by voice help at any time. When it comes to finding information, getting directions, making calls, sending texts, launching apps, and planning events, she is essential.

#### 6.6.1.2 The Tesla

Cars have grown in popularity as AI targets. Those who are interested in cars are missing out on Tesla. It is arguably the most popular vehicle on the market. The car has won many awards and is known for its self-driving capabilities, predictive abilities, and cutting-edge technological innovation.

#### 6.6.1.3 Cogito

Cogito, one of the best examples of the behavioural version to increase the intellect of customer service personnel, was co-founded by Dr. Sandy and Joshua and is currently available for purchase. The company uses behavioural science and ML to enhance phone experts’ customer participation. Every day, countless voice calls may use Cogito. AI technology tracks human speech and provides immediate feedback to help individuals behave better.

#### 6.6.1.4 Echo

Amazon released Echo, which now has additional features and is smarter. It is a cutting-edge solution that provides Alexa, a voice service to assist you with checking the web for personal data, arranging appointments, making decisions, navigating, switching, and heating and cooling systems, answering questions, reading Audible, reporting traffic and weather, giving you information about nearby businesses, offering sports schedules and ratings, and more. The remainder of the chapter covers the uses of AI and a few of its possible advantages.

 124.  

### 6.6.2 Business Applications of AI and ML: Present Uses and Upcoming Prospects

The way businesses analyse data and decide is being transformed by the growing integration of AI and ML. Businesses utilise such tools to improve client relations, streamline repetitive operations, and perform statistical analysis. Businesses may optimise operations like managing stock, service delivery, and advertising efforts by using algorithms that use ML to find undetected patterns in large datasets. A major shift in business methods is exemplified by the trend towards data-driven decision-making, which is changing sectors such as manufacturing, banking, and retail \[[24](#ch06.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp006_ref24)\]. The potential of AI and ML in business is bright. The use of NLP to improve customer interactions and the expansion of AI-powered targeted marketing solutions are examples of recent advancements. As ML models advance, businesses will give responsible AI practices more thought, guaranteeing data transparency and dependability. Additionally, these tools will become more widely available owing to advanced ML frameworks, allowing smaller businesses to employ AI or levelling the playing field \[[25](#ch06.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp006_ref25)\].

### 6.6.3 The Significance of Ethical AI

*   **Influence on Society:** The widespread use of ML (AI) systems and their significant social influence cannot be emphasised. They have the power to fundamentally shift the economy, modify political environments, and change cultural norms. Such transformative potential necessitates a corresponding dedication to ethical supervision of AI since its unchecked development may have unexpected repercussions and disproportionately affect those with limited resources.
*   **Conscientious Innovation:** Ethical AI is a prerequisite for responsible innovation and serves as a moral guide. The idea of “dual use” technological advances draws attention to the fact that AI has the capacity to be a tool that may be applied both positively and negatively \[[26](#ch06.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp006_ref26)\]. Through ethical innovation, we hope to optimise AI’s potential benefits while minimising any potential downsides.

### 6.6.4 Frameworks and Principles of Ethics

*   **Fairness:** A fundamental ethical value in AI is fairness, which requires AI to treat every person equally. However, achieving fairness is a challenging endeavour that is hampered by issues concerning algorithmic bias, data gathering, and the establishment of fairness criteria. Since algorithmic equity aims to address the deeply ingrained biases found in many AI systems, it demands thorough evaluation.
*   **Explainability and Transparency:** Both of these are essential components of AI ethics. The black-box nature of deep learning models needs more research because opaque AI systems have the potential to erode trust and  125. responsibility. Therefore, in addition to becoming morally obligatory, seeking model clearness and transparency is essential for promoting the broader adoption of AI.
*   **Accountability:** In the context of AI, accountability includes both criminal responsibility and an ethical duty to fix the harm caused by AI. Demonstrating duty in thinking is a difficult task since artificially intelligent systems behave in a distributed style. Two moral frameworks that provide useful perspectives on AI accountability are consequentialism and deontology.
*   **Security of information and privacy:** Privacy, a basic human right, presents significant ethical challenges in the age of AI. The way AI systems gather, retain, and use data raises complicated questions about knowledge, anonymisation, and information protection. With the aid of legislation like the General Privacy Regulation (GDPR), significant attempts have been made to protect the privacy of individuals in AI applications.

### 6.6.5 AI and ML’s Ethical Challenges

*   **Discrimination and Bias:** AI bias presents a significant ethical issue. This bias may be derived from historical data, which might then embed prejudices from culture into algorithms and result in adverse effects. These issues occur in a number of situations, such as hiring decisions, justice systems, and lending practices. Ethical considerations have to solve systemic injustices.
*   **Economic Impact and Employment Displacement:** The ethical implications of AI have an impact on the economy as well, specifically in terms of employment displacement. AI-driven automation raises questions about society’s responsibility to displaced workers and puts certain job sectors at risk. Widespread retraining programs and universal basic income (UBI) are two ethical strategies for reducing these effects.
*   **Autonomous Decision-Making:** The growing use of AI in autonomous decision-making, such as in medical diagnosis or driverless cars, raises difficult ethical questions. As demonstrated by the “trolley conundrum” in robot cars and the ethical dilemmas presented by AI in hospitals, it is imperative that we set ethical boundaries for AI’s autonomous choices. The urge to align AI’s ideas with our own lies at the heart of these difficulties.

## 6.7 Future Trends in AI, ML, and IoT

*   **Integration of Edge Technology:** To analyse data faster and make judgements instantly, use edge computing rather than cloud-centric processing. Use local data processing to reduce latency and improve privacy.
*   **Explainable AI, or XAI:** XAI is the development of open AI models to increase trust and responsibility. Verify if ML algorithms are understandable.
*   ** 126. Federated Learning:** ML that collaborates without sharing raw data among scattered devices, promoting security and privacy while improving model accuracy.
*   **5G Convergence with IoT:** Greater connectivity that enables smooth communication between IoT devices is known as 5G convergence with IoT. It facilitates real-time data sharing and supports innovative applications like self-driving vehicles and smart cities.
*   **AI-Powered IoT Automation:** AI integration for proactive maintenance in IoT devices, self-healing networks, and workflow automation.
*   **Blockchain-Based IoT Safety:** Blockchain technology is used to ensure data integrity and safeguard IoT, making IoT ecosystems more transparent and trustworthy.
*   **Blockchain-Driven IoT Security:** IoT is protected and data consistency is guaranteed through the use of blockchain technology, increasing the transparency and reliability of IoT ecosystems.
*   **Conscientious IoT and AI Ethics:** To address social concerns, there is a growing emphasis on creating AI in a moral way and implementing IoT devices sensibly.

## 6.8 Conclusion

By creating new opportunities for interaction, automation, and decision-making, IoT, deep learning (ML), and AI are revolutionising different sectors. Themes including edge computing, learning federation, XAI, and IoT-5G integration are influencing these technologies’ future. However, their quick acceptance also raises significant ethical concerns about privacy, algorithmic bias, safety hazards, and environmental preservation. To solve these problems, policymakers, technological professionals, and users must take a proactive and collaborative stance. Open administration, user-focused designs, and ethical frameworks are essential for ensuring that technological advancements align with social values. Finding a balance between innovation and moral responsibility will enable the creation of systems that are hospitable, safe, and sustainable. By encouraging multidisciplinary cooperation and transparency, AI, ML, and IoT may realise all their capabilities and offer revolutionary benefits while upholding the interests of individuals and communities globally.

# 7 Voice Assisted Chat Bots in Enhancing CRM in Modern Marketing for Small Businesses

Aditi Srivastava, Ajay Singh, and Brij Mohan Singh

DOI: [10.1201/9781003561873-7](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781003561873-7)

## 7.1 Introduction

Artificial intelligence (AI) and machine learning (ML) have completely changed the digital world and brought about real, observable improvements that have increased the effectiveness of the digital world. Presently dominating the market, AI and ML are predicted to transform the sector radically in the coming year \[[1](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref1)\]. The introduction of voice assistant chatbots has made the expansion of small businesses via digital marketing efforts even more noticeable \[[2](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref2)\]. Any marketing strategy that focuses on how businesses deftly and successfully maintain long-term relationships with their consumers and persuade them to make future purchases must include customer relationships as a key component \[[3](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref3)\]. Customer relationship management (CRM) is a crucial component of business and indicates consumer loyalty when they are pleased with the offering. Having strong and long-lasting relationships with consumers is essential to defining CRM since it persuades them to make additional purchases and creates space for future success in sales. CRM is monitored by systems; nowadays, AI and ML are used to monitor them. Fuzzy data technology has been used in AI and ML for advancements in research \[[4](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref4)\]. These two technologies allow for greater fusion and excellent service when they are used for operation and promotion. Small businesses have first-mover advantage benefits if they can adopt advanced AI-enabled chatbots on their websites \[[5](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref5)\].

Understanding consumer preferences and choices for Internet services and features has become more crucial for marketers thanks to the Internet \[[6](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref6)\]. The usage of technology has increased the population of the technology market in 2023 (technologies and 2021, 123AD). Over 5.4 million people used technology to accomplish their tasks in 2023 as per Internet World Stats (2020) (statistics (itu. int)). Technology is a must in the digital age to live. People may now socialize, remain in touch, and have easy access owing to technology. It is hard for us to envision our lives without technology. Our lives are now more dependable and easier owing to technology. Since technology has emerged as an essential tool, it is vital to know how consumers use it daily and how confident they feel when using online platforms. People in the digital  130. age have ready access to technology. Introducing these technologies has resulted in a significant shift in customer engagement. Technology has enabled people to select from a wide variety of products available and select at their convenience in the long run. Good technology-driven platforms are likely to attract more people by promoting engagement. The tech-savvy behaviour of users even motivates them to purchase beyond their basic needs. A customer’s comfort level with technology has a significant impact on their behavior, especially in the context of customer experience (CE). It’s critical that using technology to improve work be accepted. Various models like the Technology Acceptance Model (TAM), the Theory of Planned Behavior (TPB), and the Elaboration Likelihood Model (ELM) are considered the most effective theories and help study technology’s influence on customers \[[7](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref7), [8](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref8), [9](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref9)\].

In the digital age, it is essential to comprehend and adjust to technology. Technology has enabled people and businesses to evolve and develop strategies to sustain themselves in the market as per the advancements \[[10](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref10), [11](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref11)\]. Many clients avoid using online platforms despite their ease of use and lack of cost because they are afraid of technology or have a phobia of it. It is essential to ascertain if people utilize technology freely and how confident they feel after doing so. The likelihood of being back to the same platform for repurchases is increased by the confidence gained via online shopping. In the digital realm, a platform that exudes confidence and dependability is highly valued. The analyst focuses on how to retain customers and ensure they return to the same platform. Platforms which are driven by customization are preferred over platforms which lack customization. Technology has even enabled users to customize their purchase behaviour as per their needs and requirements.

Social media platforms provide greater viability for small businesses and e-commerce platforms to carry out businesses in the long run. The wide reach has enabled businesses to focus on social media platforms for making purchase decisions. Different models have been developed over time to study how technology plays a vital role in making purchases on e-commerce platforms. Customers’ purchasing habits have been dramatically altered by the early adoption of the TAM and the adoption of the UTAUT model.

AI and ML are tools used to analyze consumer engagement and expectations on e-commerce platforms. This study utilizes the UTAUT model adopted from the three most famous theories: TAM, TPB, and TRA \[[12](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref12)\]. Previous research has concentrated on various UTAUT models and technology model extensions from various angles. Every study has concentrated on the customers’ perception of technology, how easily it has made consumers tech-savvy, and how it has encouraged them to use it more frequently. It is even more important to understand how technology and digital platforms play a vital role in influencing and persuading the shopping behaviours and experience of customers for electronic products.

Small business firms have seen a great hike in sales in recent years; therefore, it is critical to see how technology and its acceptance are important for purchase on e-commerce platforms for this study. Given how frequently technology is used in all spheres of life and how well it raises our spirits when performing our duties, we must comprehend the technology acceptance model. One must be able to use technology freely and perceive its benefits to accept it. After using technology, the performance increases and the procedure becomes much simpler. Technology-enabled tasks are  131. preferred, and they boost the confidence of the user. This saves the time of the users. They feel more confident and tech-savvy after using these platforms.

The AI-enabled chatbot model highlights the fact that a great platform with an effective and efficient system is necessary to facilitate the purchase and repurchase of products from that platform \[[13](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref13)\]. A more efficient platform ensures less complexity and more output-based performance, especially when consumers do not want to spend more time navigating between pages in a confused state \[[14](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref14)\]. The present research is significant and to a great extent contributes to the existing research by focusing on the small business product segment which has seen a rise in demand and sales over the decade because of the increased use of social platforms. Social influencer marketing popularly known as (SIM) has led development and growth of social media promoting cost-effectiveness. An increase in demand and change in trends every quarter motivate the present generation to shop more small business products. Good discounts and festivity seasons promote more sales on such platforms; therefore, it is essential to know the digital platform and have good knowledge of latest technologies. The research gap focuses on how much customers feel comfortable using technology to buy small business products, leading to more customer engagement.

This study evolves around the research questions to be answered to enable the industry to understand the behaviour of consumers. It is vital to understand because Gen Z is even more impulsive and wants the platform to be hustle-free for quick navigation. They are as follows:

*   RQ1. What is the applicability of the UTAUT model in today’s digital era for small business products?
*   RQ2. How effectively can the model encode the voice-based chatbots (VAC) responsible for adapting the e-commerce platform’s usability and efficiency?

In the upcoming sections, we will delve into an extensive literature review, present the framework, and outline the methodology adopted in this study. By doing so, we aspire to give a robust foundation for determining and interpreting intricate dynamics at play in the realm of voice-assisted chatbots (VAC) for e-commerce applications for small business product purchases. This is essential in industries, as such an analysis provides a greater understanding and broader perspective of businesses. The framework provides space for improvement and better strategies to support and help with business extensions.

Here, the primary focus is on studying the significance of the UTAUT model and how VAC can affect the buying behaviour for small business products. The study uses VAC as a moderator to bridge a research gap in the latest literature available \[[15](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref15)\]. The literature lacks the contribution of VAC as a moderator and how VAC moderates the buying behaviour in e-commerce platforms for electronic products. The literature highlights the use of AI, and its applications lead to better customer engagement. Advanced levels of chatbots enable better performance and enhanced customer satisfaction \[[16](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref16)\]. Chatbots are in use now and customers are repeatedly using them to interact and solve their queries of purchase. Chatbot voice assistants play an essential role in small businesses where brands need recognition and more customer engagement to promote their goodwill in the market. Small businesses need recognition and  132. growth. They need customer engagement and desire to retain customers in the long run. A delighted customer is more like a family to customers. Chatbots enable 24/7 customer support, and they enable more customization for customers. The purpose here is to investigate the following objectives:

1.  To determine and understand the applicability of AI and its enhanced use in small businesses, such as voice chatbots.
2.  To study the association of all the factors used in the UTAUT model in determining CE in electronic products in e-commerce platforms.
3.  To study the moderating role of VAC and its effects on all constructs in the UTAUT model.

### 7.2 UTAUT Model and Its Elements

The UTAUT theory is one of the most predominant and effective among the eight theories on technology and innovation because it has simplified and enhanced the whole construct into four main elements and four moderating factors that affect customers \[[17](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref17)\]. The UTAUT theory ought to be the most appropriate for understanding the behaviour of consumers and how they adopt technology in their behaviour \[[18](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref18)\]. The UTAUT model comprises the most researched constructs and has produced the most significant results recently \[[19](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref19), [20](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref20), [21](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref21)\]. UTAUT is identified as potentially persuading people’s CEs to be tech-savvy \[[21](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref21)\]. The elements are specifically identified to study the customers’ behaviour and how they react to technology \[[15](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref15), [22](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref22)\]. This theory has used three elements: performance expectancy, effort expectancy, and social influence (SI) as influencing factors in determining the CE of users \[[23](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref23), [24](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref24)\]. Experience and gender cannot be taken in this study since they have already been applied to other studies \[[25](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref25), [26](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref26)\]. The EE and PE constructs have a more significant effect on CE in the fintech industry as per the literature \[[27](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref27)\]. The four constructs have also been tested successfully in learning and management systems and show their substantial associations in the model \[[12](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref12)\]. Technology-friendly customers are more likely to use technology \[[6](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref6), [28](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref28), [29](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref29), [30](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref30)\] and online platforms than less tech-savvy customers ([Im et al., 2011](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref31)) \[[31](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref31)\]. To understand the behaviour, the UTAUT model is popularly adopted to study customer psychology \[[13](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref13), [17](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref17), [23](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref23), [32](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref32)\]. The UTAUT model comprises four constructs that are used in this study and are discussed below. Popular literature focuses on these constructs to study the behaviour of customers in using technology \[[28](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref28)\] and the extent to which customers feel friendly in using these technology-driven platforms \[[31](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref31), [33](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref33)–[36](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref36)\].

#### 7.2.1 PE and CE

PE is termed as the frequency to which a consumer believes that employing tech will enable them to perform more efficiently, hence strengthening their belief in using technology. The author has also found that among the factors, PE is the strongest and most important construct influencing the behaviour and usage of technology \[[37](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref37)\].

*   H1: PE considerably affects the CE in e-commerce apps for small business products.

 133.  

#### 7.2.2 EE and CE

EE is the frequency to which a user finds using tech to be simpler \[[38](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref38)–[40](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref40)\]. When discussing many industries, such as health care and mobile uptake, it is regarded as a crucial component \[[41](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref41), [42](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref42)\].

*   H2: EE directly affects the CE in e-commerce applications for small business products (Rahi et al.) \[[28](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref28)\].

#### 7.2.3 SI and CE

At present, SI is considered essential since it compels individuals to adjust to contemporary technologies \[[43](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref43)\]. Everywhere, people embrace and value tech-savvy users on a social level, and vice versa \[[44](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref44)\]. People are frequently compelled by society and social pressure to adopt newer technologies, and they do so frequently \[[39](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref39)\]. SI has a significant impact on users because of the digital era ([Lu, Yao, and Yu, 2005](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref36)) \[[36](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref36)\]. Different sectors like health care have seen significant effects of SI on users \[[39](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref39), [42](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref42)\].

*   H3: SI significantly affects the CE in e-commerce apps for small business products.

#### 7.2.4 FC and CE

FC has a direct and substantial influence on users’ CEs and intentions for using technology.

H4: FC significantly affects the CE in e-commerce apps for small business products \[[45](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref45)\].

#### 7.2.5 CE and CRM

In numerous fields where CE is found to be a strong predictor of consumer behaviour utilizing technology, numerous studies have placed a strong emphasis on CE and CRM \[[46](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref46)\].

*   H5: CE of users considerably impacts the CE that motivates users to adapt e-commerce applications for small business products \[[47](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref47)\].

#### 7.2. 6 Voice-Assisted Chatbots and Customer Engagement

VAC in AI are a new concept and enabling such features in platforms for small businesses will promote small businesses and help them have good brand infirmity for customers. There have been many developments in AI using ML to promote websites \[[48](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref48)\]. VAC provide better CE and provide larger exposure to customers to engage in websites more precisely. They are guided by chatbots very effectively and are recommended products that fit the best of their abilities. With emerging AI, VAC are the future of shopping, enhancing the journey of customers through a new lens.

## 7.3 Research Methodology

The chapter focuses on how AI will enable customer engagement using the UTAUT model in small businesses. The UTAUT model is adopted to know the applicability of AI in online websites. UTAUT is a very famous research model for studying and understanding customer perspectives and how customer engagement is created for small business models. The study uses AI-enabled voice assistants like chatbots and their enhanced uses to gain customer engagement. The chapter is empirical and includes framing of a questionnaire to be filled by people who are actively using small businesses for their shopping needs. The data is collected using an online Google form. Purposive sampling was used in the study because only those respondents who purchased from small businesses were contacted as shown in [Figure 7.1](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872-007-Figure-001). The sample size of data collection was approximately 690 and SEM was used in the study to understand customer engagement using AI-enabled VAC shown in [Figure 7.2](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872-007-Figure-002). The questionnaire was divided into two sections where the first section included the demographic profile of the respondent and the second section included information on AI-enabled voice assistants used in purchases from small businesses. Chatbots are more trustworthy and enable the building of goodwill and trust for companies \[[49](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref49)\].

### 7.3.1 Instrument Development

The items of the questionnaire were constructed using previous works of literature and they were modified. There were two sections in the questionnaire. To create scales for the use of PE, EE, SI, FC, and CE, the original items from Ref. \[[40](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref40)\] were changed and fitted to e-commerce. The experience was also gauged by how frequently e-commerce mobile applications were used. The academic professionals with relevant experience were shown the questionnaire’s final iteration. Their input had a vital role in enhancing the clarity of the questionnaire. In the second section, there were two stages in which the data was collected. The primary stage of the study consisted of questions that demonstrated the moderating role of the four constructs of the UTAUT model and the mediating variable CE to purchase. The first stage focused on how the moderator variable VAC moderates the association between the IV and CE as a moderator. The first stage also utilized demographic profiling of the respondents, such as age, gender, and occupation, and was gathered in the first part. The questions were framed using the 5-point Likert scale, which ranged from 1 being “strongly disagree” to 5 being “strongly agree.”

![Figure 7.2 Structural model. Return to text.⏎](Images/Figure7_2.jpg)

Figure 7.2 Structural model. [Return to text.⏎](#ch07.xhtml#R_24032-0872-007-Figure-002)

 135.  

### 7.3.2 Data Analysis

We used partial least squares (PLS) regression to analyze the offered research framework, just like the original UTAUT investigations \[[38](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref38)\] did. The PLS, created by Herman World in the 1960s, is a practical exploratory analysis tool and likely the least constricting of the multiple linear regression extension \[[50](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref50)\]. It is beneficial for building predictive models where there may be collinearity among factors. Literature on PLS regression’s benefits and drawbacks can be found \[[51](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref51)\]. Factor loadings, composite reliability, and average variance extracted (AVE) were used to evaluate the convergent validities, as recommended by Refs \[[34](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref34), [52](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref52)\], while the discriminant validity was assessed by determining whether or not the square root of AVE exceeded the correlation between constructs, as recommended by Ref. \[[38](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref38)\], and the reliability was assessed by evaluating internal consistency reliability.

### 7.3.3 Measurement Model

In this stage of the PLS-SEM approach, the measurement model is used to ensure the degree and rationality of the study, confirming their reliability, convergent validity, and discriminant validity. Using Cronbach’s alpha and composite reliability, the internal consistency and reliability of the construct measurements were assessed \[[53](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref53)–[57](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref57)\]. As displayed in [Table 7.1](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_ch007_tab_001), all Cronbach’s alpha and composite reliability values exceeded the acceptable threshold of 0.70, as suggested by Ref. \[[58](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref58)\], confirming the robustness and reliability of all measures.

Following the guidelines outlined by Ref. \[[59](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref59)\], convergent validity was assessed by examining the standardized factor loadings and AVE. To establish convergent validity, it was necessary to ensure that (i) all measurement items exceeded a threshold of 0.70, (ii) the composite reliability was above 0.70, and (iii) AVE was greater than 0.50 \[[59](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref59), [60](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref60)\]. This study met all these criteria, as indicated in [Table 7.2](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_ch007_tab_002). Consequently, the findings strongly confirmed its convergent validity.

To establish discriminant validity, an assessment was performed by comparing the square roots of AVE with the correlations among constructs to confirm that each factor was distinct or uncorrelated \[[61](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref61)–[66](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref66)\]. As shown in [Table 7.2](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_ch007_tab_002), all shared variances among factors within the model were lower than the square root of the AVE, indicating dissimilarity between the constructs. The findings revealed a lack of substantial correlation among variables in the model, affirming the absence of multicollinearity in the study, which is consistent with the observations of Ref. \[[60](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref60)\].

### 7.3.4 Structural Model and Hypotheses Testing

The second step in the PLS-SEM methodology involves evaluating the structural model. This evaluation analyzes the standardized beta coefficients and *t*\-values associated with the model’s hypotheses. Independent variables, including social media influencer attractiveness, social media platform reputation, and content appeal, were studied in the model. Moreover, informational social influence and normative social influence intention were considered mediators, and impulsive buying behaviour was the dependent variable within the model.

Hypothesis H[1] determined whether *PE considerably affects the usage of voice-assisted chatbots*. The relation is positive here. The results presented in [Table 7.3](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_ch007_tab_003) revealed that performance expectancy played a substantial role in predicting the usage of VAC within this context (β = 0.37, *p* < 0.05), confirming hypothesis H[1]. Hypothesis H[2] examined whether *EE positively affects the usage of voice-assisted chatbots*. [Table 7.3](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_ch007_tab_003) demonstrates that effort expectancy does have a substantial impact on the use of VAC (β = 0.45, *p* < 0.05), supporting hypothesis H[2].

Hypothesis H[3] tested whether SI *affects the usage of voice-assisted chatbots*. The relation is positive here. According to the results obtained from PLS-SEM, it was determined that SI significantly influenced the usage of VAC, with a β value of 0.34 and a *p*\-value less than 0.05. Consequently, hypothesis H[3] was validated. Hypothesis H[4] determined whether *FC positively affects the usage of voice-assisted chatbots*. The outcomes indicated that facilitating conditions played a substantial role in predicting user behaviour, with a β value of 0.24 and a *p*\-value less than 0.05. Therefore, hypothesis H[4] was supported. Hypothesis H[5] tested whether the *use of voice-assisted chatbots positively affects customer engagement.* The robustness of the connections between the two factors offered confirmation for this association (β = 0.67, *p* < 0.05). Therefore, hypothesis H[5] is validated.

## 7.4 Findings

The results show that PE has a significant positive impact on the use of VAC (β = 0.37, *p* < 0.05). This confirms that users prefer to adopt the chatbot if they assume it to be useful and effective in enhancing their performance, thereby supporting H[1].

 139. EF has the highest impact on the adoption of VAC, with a β value of 0.45 (*p* < 0.05). This finding supports H[2], indicating that when people find the chatbot easy to use, they are more inclined to adopt it.

The model confirmed that SI considerably affects the usage of VAC (β = 0.34, *p* < 0.05). Hypothesis H[3] was validated, suggesting that people’s usage decisions are affected by social factors, such as peers, instructors, or social norms regarding technology adoption.

FC, as per the model, positively impacts people’s use of the chatbot (β = 0.24, *p* < 0.05), confirming H[4]. This demonstrates that the availability of appropriate technical support and environment plays a significant role in encouraging the use of the chatbot.

The study revealed that the use of the VAC significantly enhances customer engagement (β = 0.67, *p* < 0.05). Hypothesis H[5] was validated, indicating a strong association between chatbot usage and increased engagement levels.

## 7.5 Discussion

The analysis underscores the relevance of various factors in predicting the incorporation of VAC. PE and EE emerged as the vital predictors, which aligns with the principles of the UTAUT model. Users prefer to use the chatbot if they find it as both beneficial to their tasks and highly adaptable to operate. The effective role of SI recommends that peer recommendations, social norms, and faculty endorsements can heavily influence users’ behaviour to incorporate new technologies. This points to the importance of promoting chatbot usage through community and group activities, possibly leveraging word-of-mouth strategies or testimonials from influential peers or instructors. FC also proved to be an essential factor in chatbot adoption, emphasizing the need for institutions to provide adequate resources and support for people to use these tools effectively. Ensuring that people have access to the necessary infrastructure can significantly reduce potential barriers to adoption. Finally, the study confirmed that the use of VAC enhances customer engagement. This highlights the potential of such technologies not only for academic purposes but also for fostering deeper interactions and relationships with people, improving overall customer satisfaction and involvement.

## 7.6 Conclusion

This chapter focused on how VAC lead to better customer engagement, ultimately leading to a better customer relationship manager for small business products. The study specifically focused on small business products to help them grow and expand exponentially in the long run. Since now small business products are evolving, small businesses need to incorporate AI in their applications with advanced levels of customer engagement tools. Good customer engagement leads to good customization of websites and applications which eventually leads to better customer retention leading to customer relationship building in the long run. Voice-assisted chatbots enable great customer engagement and they automatically guide customers through their purchase journey, leading to better purchase preferences.

 140.  

## 7.7 Theoretical and Practical Implications

This chapter aims to seek how AI has again transformed digital marketing and how it is benefiting small businesses in growing globally. Small businesses have evolved as one of the rapidly growing businesses with the advent of the Shark Tank India and Make in India initiatives. Small businesses are growing exponentially and are preferred by the people \[[67](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref67)–[69](#ch07.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp007_ref69)\]. With the inclusion of an application-based system, which AI drives, it is even more commendable that these businesses have greater opportunities in the market for growth. The emergence of VAC enables consumers to explore the applications more rapidly. VAC enables easier navigation through websites and how these websites retain customers for longer duration in the e-commerce platforms. A long retention strategy in a website enables consumer purchase. A consumer who is likely to stay more on websites is likely to purchase. This is essential for small businesses to maintain greater visibility in the market. Small businesses design their e-commerce websites as per the customers. Customization in the form of VAC enables a more customer-engaged website. A website that has more customer engagement can build CRM. Good customer engagement can build effective customer relationships. Small businesses need effective CRM for long-term sustainability in the market. Therefore, VAC enable customers to interact with the websites of small businesses more effectively, leading to building of brand image for small businesses. VAC enable searching for products more easily. Customers ask chatbots without typing about products they search for. Chatbots are AI-driven applications that analyze customer behaviour on web platforms and recommend the most suitable products with effective pricing in a couple of seconds. Chatbots assist them personally to have better products in their carts. For small businesses, it is essential to have this feature on their platforms to enable a larger customer engagement, resulting in larger audiences who they cater to. Designing smart applications and websites with smart VAC provides a wider shopping perspective. Customers can use them to navigate the website without even typing. The VAC will analyze more aggressively the behaviour of users.

## 7.8 Limitations and Future Research

The chapter is practical and is based on innovation; however, it also has some limitations, creating a scope for further research. First, the concept is confined to the UTAUT model. Many other theories have also evolved in management guiding. Secondly, convenience sampling is used for data collection since only those respondents who were conveniently available were considered. In future research, this can also be carried out through random sampling where every participant has a chance. Many other factors are also responsible for promoting small business products using AI, ML, and IoT. Thirdly, future research can inculcate location tracking with advanced voice chatbots that can automatically evaluate the customer’s location and recommend the products of small businesses in nearby areas.

# 8 IOT Applications in Business Operations

Taher Alkhalaf, Omar Durrah, and Monir Alkhalaf

DOI: [10.1201/9781003561873-8](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781003561873-8)

## 8.1 Introduction

The Internet of Things (IoT) has received widespread attention from academics and business owners and is now seen as one of the innovative technologies of the twentieth century and a technological means that helps in creating new business models for products/services in various economic sectors ([Ibarra-Esquer et al., 2017](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref35)). [Atzori et al. (2010)](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref9) argue that the crucial element of IoT is its ability to influence people’s behavior and daily lives at home and at work ([Roopa et al., 2019](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref55)). Therefore, potential applications of IoT can be found in many areas including health, education, and security ([Jain and Jain, 2022](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref38)). Likewise, at the commercial level, it can shape automation in production, logistics, operations management, and the supply chain of goods, for example. As a result, IoT is expected to have crucial impacts on economic and social development worldwide. Borgia claims that IoT is an emerging model of a series of interconnected objects that form a global network and that the large number of devices that are interconnected and capable of collecting vast amounts of information can provide opportunities for the creation of new services that bring benefits to people, society, the environment, and the economy ([Borgia, 2014](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref14)).

It is expected to be ubiquitous in everyday life and provide everyday services through sensors, communication systems, and digital processing so that machines operate automatically and human intervention will remain limited ([Chen, 2012](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref19); [Li et al., 2015a](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref47)).

The collection and analysis of real-time data from desktops, laptops, tablets, and smartphones create an incredible value for information ([Bhushan and Agrawal, 2020](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref11)), providing deep insights to decision makers. When sophisticated data analysis algorithms are used, highly accurate forecasts can be made and insights can be provided for any event, including health care, weather, transportation, agriculture, manufacturing, and the stock market ([Grover et al., 2024](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref27)). Based on these insights, information can be viewed as a significant economic asset for companies ([Alkhalaf and Badewi, 2024](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref6)) that may appear on a company’s balance sheet ([Greengard, 2015](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref26)). The McKinsey Institute estimates that the availability of data reduces operating costs by 50% or more and may prompt companies to rethink the way they manage their human and material investments ([Manyika, 2011](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref49)).

The term “Internet of Things” was coined in 1999 by technology pioneer Kevin Ashton at the MIT Auto-ID Center in the radio frequency identification (RFID) community, as a system that connects the Internet to the physical world through the use  147. of computers, routers, and sensors in the physical environment without direct human intervention ([Ashton, 2009](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref8)) and simultaneously monitors and identifies objects in the real world ([Gupta and Quamara, 2020](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref30)). Ashton argues that these devices can generate, capture, and record data with greater accuracy than humans in the real world, since human behavior is limited by time, attention, and accuracy in capturing data ([Joshi and Gupta, 2019](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref40)), pointing out the importance of the physical environment and that the economy and societies depend not only on information but also on things ([Ashton, 2009](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref8)). Research indicates that although the idea of IoT emerged during this period, a large group of technologies that assist or are part of its design or support have been spreading and developing side by side for many years, such as sensor nodes, including wireless communication technologies and digital devices capable of collecting, processing, and connecting data to the Internet for many sectors ([Samih, 2019](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref56); [Akyildiz et al., 2002](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref2)).

Some researchers describe IoT as the interconnection of electronic devices, referred to as a group of objects or sensor nodes, connected to the Internet in a physical world without human intervention. Kevin Ashton expects that RFID technology will lead to the creation of a unified way for computers and their associated objects to operate, which will help in the complete automation of supply chains, understanding the real world, and better performance in terms of inventory management, in addition to enhancing and supporting the customer experience ([Akyildiz et al., 2002](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref2)). In this sense, this design is described as a seamless, available, and inexpensive system that will automatically connect physical objects to the global Internet, subject to international and governmental standards so that it can be applied and benefited by commercial and industrial companies and individuals in their ordinary lives ([Qin et al., 2016](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref54)).

## 8.2 Applications of IoT

The concept of IoT can be understood through the field in which it is applied. Things are in themselves products, equipment, means of transportation, and storage that are linked to the life cycle of the product ([Munirathinam, 2020](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref50)). Thus, in some aspects, it represents an extension of the interaction between people and the surrounding environment. In general, the identified areas of applications of IoT are shown in [Figure 8.1](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872-008-Figure-001).

Applications provide the ability to run system functions and interact with external objects via Web Service Description Language (WSDL) and Business Process Definition Language (BPEL) ([Atzori et al., 2010](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref9)).

[Sundmaeker et al. (2010)](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref61) and [Gupta and Quamara (2020)](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref30) suggested several main areas of possible applications for IoT: In industry, IoT helps in providing information to start industrial work such as providing data on inventory, purchase, product, equipment, and quantities of any product in the workshops and even after it is transported to the stores. IoT can be applied in the environment to measure rainfall, water trees, and monitor buildings. Finally, IoT can be connected to places designated for entertainment, sports, restaurants, and residential complexes (see [Table 8.1](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_ch008_tab_001)).

According to [Atzori et al. (2010)](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref9), the applications are grouped into the following five areas.

![Figure 8.1 Application domains and relevant major scenarios.Source: Atzori et al., 2010, p. 2794. Return to text.⏎](Images/Figure8_1.jpg)

 148.  

Table 8.1 IoT Application Domains [Return to text.⏎](#ch08.xhtml#R_24032-0872_ch008_tab_001)

 
| **Domain** || **Description** || **Indicative examples** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Industry** || Activities involving financial or commercial transactions between companies, organisations and other entities || Manufacturing, logistics, service sector, banking, financial governmental authorities, intermediaries, etc. |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Environment** || Activities regarding the protection, monitoring and development of all natural resources || Agriculture and breeding, recycling, environmental management services, energy management, etc. |
| **Society** || Activities/initiatives regarding the development and inclusion of and people societies, and people || Governmental service towards citizens and other society structures (e-participation), e-inclusion (e.g. again disabled people), etc. |

### 8.2.1 IoT and Supply Chain Management

IoT has revolutionized many fields, helping companies move to Industry 4.0 ([Ben-Daya et al., 2019](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref10); [de Vass et al., 2021a](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref21); [Biswal et al., 2023](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref13)) or the so-called Industrial Internet of Things (IIoT) which connects technical objects and things in industrial  149. production lines through which ongoing processes can be controlled continuously ([Boyes et al., 2018](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref17)). Modern business technologies have significantly impacted logistics management and the nature of supply chain operations by providing communications and data transfer that enhance supply chain performance ([de Vass et al., 2021b](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref22); [Durrah et al., 2023](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref25)). In this context, IoT can provide real-time data to decision makers, starting from production and design decisions, purchasing supplies, raw material inventory, operating processes, finished goods, and expected transportation time. This technology can bring significant benefits in the field of demand forecasting, warehousing, and cost reduction ([Bose and Pal, 2005](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref15); [Alkhalaf, 2006](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref5)).

Owing to IoT, transportation vehicles, roads, and railways have become equipped with sensor technologies that allow the collection of information to direct traffic and provide information to drivers about road conditions and choose appropriate routes to relieve congestion, allowing for highly efficient traffic management ([Atzori et al., 2010](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref9); [Li et al., 2006](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref46)). Internal integration of supply chains has been found to improve performance, deliver value to customers, and deliver services at the lowest possible cost and at the fastest possible speed ([Shee et al., 2018](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref59)). However, despite the positive results and benefits that companies can achieve and the low cost and easy installation of IoT devices, companies are still hesitant to invest in this technology ([Tu, 2018](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref63)) for reasons related to data privacy, the complexity of implementing this technology, potential financial burdens, and social implications within the workforce ([Haddud et al., 2017](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref31)).

### 8.2.2 IoT and Healthcare

IoT technologies can be applied in healthcare, starting from diagnosis to monitoring and treatment ([Jain and Jain, 2022](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref38)). Moreover, through sensor systems, many vital functions of the human body, such as temperature, cholesterol levels, and blood pressure levels can be measured ([Jain and Jain, 2022](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref38)). The technologies can be used for prevention and remote monitoring, allowing communication with doctors, emergency services, and nurses. These technologies can help patients through touch-screen devices that allow patients to operate some devices such as the Internet and TV, or follow lessons if the patients are of school age ([Borgia, 2014](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref14); [Delmastro, 2012](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref23)). The process of tracking patients helps reduce accidents and human errors in the field of healthcare, especially in providing medication doses at the right time, and the most obvious example is tracking the identity of births in hospitals to prevent errors associated with such cases ([Sharma et al., 2020](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref58)). In addition, automatic tracking helps in managing the inventory of medicines and equipment and indicates the pressure sites in clinics to transfer patients to other departments ([Atzori et al., 2010](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref9)).

### 8.2.3 IoT and Smart environment

Today, it is possible to connect homes with smart technologies that provide useful services to people ([Sharma et al., 2020](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref58)). They may provide comfort to consumers and facilitate their daily lives, such as automating the operation of washing machines, televisions, and lighting ([Jain and Jain, 2022](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref38)). Moreover, it has become possible to  150. use IoT for monitoring, security, and safety in homes, electrical systems, and air conditioning ([Callaghan and Hagras, 2010](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref18)).

### 8.2.4 IoT and Personal and Social

Owing to IoT technologies and related applications, it has become possible to communicate between people through social networks and interact from anywhere and at any time ([Samih, 2019](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref56)). Personal Internet of Things (PIoT) use sensors to manage things in homes and consumer appliances, such as air-conditioners, or through IoT even personal devices such as smartphones and mobile devices ([Gulzar et al., 2024](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref29)). [Biswa et al. (2021)](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref12) define PIoT as “a group of connected devices focused mainly in homes and the immediate proximity of an individual” (p. 1).

IoT has also helped in providing social communication between users through what is known as the Social Internet of Things (SIoT) ([Roopa et al., 2019](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref55); [Afzal et al., 2019](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref1)), which can enhance interaction between people and provide reliable services using social networks, such as LinkedIn, Plazes, Facebook, TikTok, and X play, which play a major role in allowing people to communicate and update about their work, travel, and daily life ([Atzori et al., 2010](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref9)).

## 8.3 Development of IoT

### 8.3.1 Early Attempts at IoT

Researchers and practitioners have provided insights into smart devices that can connect things together, including machine-to-machine (M2M), RFID, wearable medical devices, and the Web of Things ([Press, 2014](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref53)). According to [La Diega (2023)](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref43), the basic idea of the “Internet of Things” traces back to Nikola Tesla in 1926, when he envisioned that mobile phones and telecommunications could turn the Earth into a brain that receives and sends neural signals. In the 1970s, a machine was developed as a “thing” to sell Coca-Cola at “Carnegie Mellon Computer Science Department,” which would signal to remote users whether it was in use or out of use ([Gulzar et al., 2024](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref29)).

### 8.3.2 The Early Visions (Late 1990s to 2005)

In the late 1990s, Bill Joy presented an early vision of IoT at the World Economic Forum in Davos, Switzerland, as a sensor system linked to the Internet that could provide useful services to subscribers (see [Table 8.2](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_ch008_tab_002)) ([Pontin, 2005](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref52)). In the second half of 1999, Ashton first coined the phrase “Internet of Things” as the title of a presentation in which he linked the use of RFID in the Procter & Gamble supply chain to the Internet. This is the first and most reliable way of using computer systems and physical objects to collect data with little human intervention ([La Diega, 2023](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref43)). Based on this vision, the International Telecommunication Union (ITU) presented an image of IoT as a virtual world that defines the identity of things in cyberspace, which facilitates communication and interaction between humans and things, or between things themselves ([ITU, 2005](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref37)).

 151.  

Table 8.2Six Webs [Return to text.⏎](#ch08.xhtml#R_24032-0872_ch008_tab_002)

 
| **Category** || **Description** |
| --- | --- |
| **Near web** || This refers to the tangible devices that are connected to the computer, such as the keyboard and the transmitter, and it refers to a physical and wireless network. |
| --- | --- |
| **Here web** || The internet that offers access from anywhere, anytime |
| **Far Web** || This refers to the internet that allows remote access like television. |
| **Weird Web** || This is the most widely used web that offers both voice and camera access. |
| **B2B** || Refers to the internet without user interfaces, providing access to machines and can be leveraged for business. |
| **D2D** || It’s an internet that sensors can open up to device-to-device access, allowing for data sharing, monitoring and control. This type of machine intelligence is still in development for use in everyday life for businesses and individuals. |

The proliferation of modern communication technologies has revolutionized the way people interact with their environment. Hence, the vision of IoT was born. The discovery of new technologies, especially in the field of wireless systems, device technologies, and service-oriented architecture, in addition to privacy issues and protocols, is an integral part of the current landscape of today’s world ([Sundmaeker et al., 2010](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref61)).

In 2005, the International Telecommunication Union (ITU) highlighted the concept of IoT by publishing the first official report on the subject. The ITU proposed that IoT is a technology that combines technological advances such as sensors, wireless networks, embedded systems that think, and nanotechnology.

### 8.3.3 The Foundation Phase (from 2009 to 2011)

In this phase, an infrastructure is created through which physical objects are connected to the Internet so that physical objects such as communications, routing, sensing, and data processing play a role in business operations. A conference on IoT was held in Zurich, Switzerland, with participation from three laboratories, University of St. Gallen, ETH Zurich, and MIT organized in Zurich to exchange knowledge and develop e-commerce with great benefits for consumers. A conference was organized in Tokyo to explore the technical challenges facing the use of IoT in the field of health, energy service for buildings, prediction of natural disasters, tracking for traffic congestion management, monitoring of products, their storage and shelf life before recycling, etc. ([Sundmaeker et al., 2010](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref61)).

### 8.3.4 A Shift toward Data and Services (from 2012 to 2020)

There have been attempts to develop communication devices by connecting things to each other, which has added new value to corporate services and helped improve  152. business processes, operations, and support customer experience ([Alam et al., 2013](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref3)). Research indicates that the ability to integrate or connect things with sensor systems will create new business models and thus innovate new services ([Skaržauskienė and Kalinauskas, 2015](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref60)). In 2015, some developments were recorded, particularly in sensing systems, where researchers began to compare these means to the five human senses and the way the brain processes information and communication ([Ibarra-Esquer et al., 2017](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref35)).

Social media platforms were developed between 2013 and 2016) via PIoT systems with the aim of communicating between people, companies, and devices. Moreover, in the period between 2016 and 2020), the cognitive Internet of Things (CIoT) was developed to further understand the interaction between the real world and the physical environment. It seeks to reach the point that devices or systems will be able to achieve self-learning, automatic thinking, and decision-making ([Gulzar et al., 2024](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref29)).

### 8.3.5 The Era of Smart Industry (IIoT)

Today, the focus is on cyber-physical smart manufacturing (CPMS), which aims to realize the independent management of production factors in industries, including available space, production planning, scheduling, storage, and real-time data acquisition ([Cheng et al., 2018](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref20)). Based on the IoT architecture, smart manufacturing can be applied to task scheduling, reliability in production quality, and monitoring of machinery and its performance, which helps improve the efficiency of the entire production process ([Li et al., 2015b](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref45); [Malik et al., 2021](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref48)).

IoT is considered to be one of the most important technologies of the Fourth Industrial Revolution (4.0), as it combines information technology, communication devices, big data, and ML for use in industries ([Zhou et al., 2015](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref68); [Khan et al., 2020](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref42)), so that things can operate independently in different environments ([Hofmann and Rüsch, 2017](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref33)), and is called the smart industry ([Greengard, 2015](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref26)). The development of the wireless communications sector and wireless sensor networks (WSNs) has contributed to reading environmental data and indicators from their various sources, which represents the beginning of a new stage in the development of the Internet as a technological revolution that can transform the Internet from Web 1.0 (WWW) to Web 2.0 (social networks) and then to Web 3.0, which is known as the global computing network ([Uckelmann et al., 2011](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref64)). There have been many attempts to connect and enrich the IoT network with multiple systems such as cloud computing, cyber systems, and monitoring devices ([Kagermann et al., 2013](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref41); [Borgia, 2014](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref14)).

Cloud computing is expected to provide, by linking processing and storage systems, a virtual platform that allows users to access services regardless of their location. Moreover, the platform can provide commercial services and the possibility of research and development. Fourth-generation technology (4G-LTE) can provide high-speed communications via the Internet platform to end users ([Gupta and Quamara, 2020](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref30)).

## 8.4 The Industrial Internet of Things (IIoT) or Industrial Internet 0.4

A new sub-sector has been created as a result of the developments in smart technologies and IoT called the Industrial Internet of Things (IIoT) which is leading the  153. Industry 4.0. This new innovation in the Internet sector focuses on automation of production processes, machine learning, and real-time data and information analysis and is expected to bring significant economic and social benefits ([Munirathinam, 2020](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref50)). [Boyes et al. (2018)](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref17) proposed a definition of Industrial Internet 4.0 that includes two key elements. Sensors are connected to machines, industrial equipment, and the Internet, in addition to connecting these objects to industrial networks capable of generating value without human intervention. The definition distinguishes between two types of networks: the traditional or consumer/social Internet, which focuses on creating value from advertising, and the industrial Internet, which creates value by increasing operational efficiency, maintaining industrial machines and equipment, and reducing costs ([Boyes et al., 2018](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref17); [Dijkman et al., 2015](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref24)).

![Figure 8.2 The difference between IoT and IIoT. Source: The Internet of Things (IoT): A practical guide. https://www.cumulocity.com/resource-library/the-internet-of-things-iot-a-practical-guide/ Return to text.⏎](Images/Figure8_2.jpg)

Figure 8.2 The difference between IoT and IIoT. Source: The Internet of Things (IoT): A practical guide. [https://www.cumulocity.com/resource-library/the-internet-of-things-iot-a-practical-guide/](https://www.cumulocity.com/resource-library/the-internet-of-things-iot-a-practical-guide/) [Return to text.⏎](#ch08.xhtml#R_24032-0872-008-Figure-002)

Some researchers and experts use the term Future Internet of Things (FIoT) to express the innovative technologies and applications in this field that may be available in the future. Another concept is currently being proposed, which is the CIoT, which is expected to be integrated with other cognitive systems so that it can make smart decisions that contribute to enhancing performance. This type of Internet may have the ability to understand types of businesses and analyze data and perceived information based on previous knowledge, and take the necessary measures to improve performance ([Zhang et al., 2015](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref67)).

[Figure 8.2](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872-008-Figure-002) shows IoT directed to direct users or consumers and IoT based on industries, illustrating the relationship between IoT and commercial companies and industries ([Malik et al., 2021](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref48)).

[World Economic Forum (2017)](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref66) described IIoT as the use of IoT technologies in manufacturing processes. In this regard, IIoT technologies help manufacturers operate in real time and analyze data to maintain flexibility and control. Some researchers and experts use the term FIoT to express the innovative technologies and applications in this field that may be available in the future ([Ibarra-Esquer et al., 2017](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref35)). [Table 8.3](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_ch008_tab_003) shows the comparison of IoT systems and IIoT systems.

## 8.5 Business Benefits of IIoT

IoT can also provide significant opportunities for stakeholders, such as industrialists in the field of developing and manufacturing devices and applications and business owners in the field of providing and maintaining Internet services ([Gupta and  154. Quamara, 2020](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref30)). The report of ITU identified challenges that could hinder the optimal exploitation of the Internet’s potential, including issues related to data privacy, standardization, and ethical and social issues, for example. However, it is expected that there will be between 50 and 100 billion connected things by 2020 and that the potential connections of things via the Internet could increase by a factor of 100 billion. [Helbing and Pournaras (2015)](#ch08.xhtml#_24032-0872_chp008_ref32) have confirmed that 150 billion things will be connected to one another and to billions of people within ten years, and that the amount of data and information will double twice a day instead of once a year as it currently does.
